  {

    "content": "/no_think\nYou are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n2\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF22.0, F20.0, F23.0, F24.0\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. I know they have been watching my moves at work. They keep changing the pallet counts on my shifts to mess up my numbers, just to keep me from getting that supervisor spot.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever seen, heard, or felt things that others around you don’t seem to notice?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean... sometimes. Like when I'm working down in the aisle, or even just at home folding clothes, I'll hear a man's voice. It's clear as day. Like when I'm working down in the aisle, or even just at home folding clothes, I'll hear a man's voice. It's clear as day. He just tells me, \"Nicole, check it again.\" But there's never anybody near me when it happens. Like they have some plan or agenda against you that you can't stop?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, like I was saying earlier about the warehouse. They definitely have a plan. My husband and the people at work keep acting like I'm crazy for saying it, but I know for a fact they're watching my every move. They're doing it on purpose—changing my pallet counts on my shifts. It's their agenda to keep me from getting that supervisor spot, and it feels like no matter how hard I work, I can't stop them from messing with my numbers.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have a special role or importance that others don't recognize, like you're meant for something bigger that other people don't understand?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so.
